Summary

After regaining some freedom, Rio Vidal has been trying to make wiser choices. Of course, she won't check out a married woman. Or kiss her in the elevator. Or have an affair with her. No, she won't. Or will she?

Or Agathario Bound (1996) AU.

Chapter 1

On any other day, Rio would have taken the stairs. Since she’d been aspiring to make wiser choices, Rio took the elevator. Unfortunately, the road to wisdom came with familiar sensations. Sweaty palms. Blurry vision. Uneven breath.

An answer before the question, a reaction before the action.

Taking a deep breath, Rio walked inside the elevator, leaning against the cold metal wall and shutting her eyes before taking herself to a place that existed only inside her mind. A technique taught by her former counselor. “Just let your mind take you to your calm place, Rio.” As if it were that easy.

The doors close in. Rio imagines an open field, a nice cottage with a lake or waterfall nearby, maybe. Some farm animals like cows. Or goats. Definitely goats. She imagines herself caressing its fur, living a peaceful, quiet, undangerous-

“Hold the elevator.”

The voice made Rio imagine a screaming goat being devoured by a wild animal. Much blood spilled. Fantasy ruined. Rio opened her eyes to see a well-dressed man approaching the elevator. She puts her foot between the doors.

Rio clears her throat and greets him with a nod, but he ignores her, bringing his phone back to his ear to continue a call. “Sir, I can promise you we can resolve this situation by February…”

Probably, he mistook Rio as the help and, considering her beaten paint- stained jeans, the three buckets of paint at her feet, and the power work tool boxes she was holding, he wasn’t entirely to be blamed for. Yet, she considered giving him some snarky comment, maybe a rude introduction. But whatever plans she had were brutally interrupted because there she was. Again. The mysterious neighbor.

During the week Rio was living in that building, they passed each other in the hallways a few times. And in none of those times did that rude woman had bothered acknowledging Rio’s existence. Probably didn’t even notice her through those damn sunglasses she always had on.

Given the close proximity of a tight space, Rio could smell her like never before. Lavender and pretty things. Rio always enjoyed pretty things and had seen plenty, but none as pretty as that woman. Even through the thick coat, Rio still could see how her ass was perfectly marked on that purple dress she was wearing beneath. Her long, wavy hair was cascading down her shoulders. Rio wanted to touch it and-

Fuck me.

While the man continued his call, the woman had now turned slightly to the left, just enough to catch Rio’s gaze directly at her breasts. Rio looks away instantly, trying her best not to blush, and refusing to meet her eyes (or her sunglasses, if you will).

Why would you choose now to acknowledge I exist?

Rio half expects her to turn back around, half expects her to complain to her husband. But she does nothing. Rio can feel that unmistakable sensation of being stared at, and when Rio finally gathers the courage to look at the woman, she is leaning against the elevator wall, much closer than before.

The first thing Rio notices is her completely open coat, meaning she can properly see her breasts now. The woman slowly removes her sunglasses, and Rio lifts her eyes to look at the most beautiful blue eyes that ever met hers. She strategically hangs the sunglasses on her dress, enhancing her neckline. Rio dumb-blinks a couple of times, like a stray cat asking for food. And then, the woman gives her a quick smirk.

And Rio? Oh, poor and naive Rio cannot help the whimper that came out of her mouth. Loud. Public. Way too loud for a public space. With an expression of confusion turned into anger, the man turns around abruptly in mid-sentence, making Rio jump and accidentally kick off one of the buckets.

“Shit,” she bends to pick it up. “I’m sorry, I didn’t mean to do that.”

“Mr. Shaw, can you hold that thought for a minute?” he hangs and continues to stare at Rio, her face now completely red.

Rio puts herself together again and looks back at him. Rio had seen that expression many times before: his evaluating, trying to decide the best way to assert his dominance.

“There is a service elevator.” he turns around again. “You better learn how to use it or I will file a complaint with the building manager.”

Rio looks at his neck and, for a few seconds, imagines how easy it would be to break it, but again: wise choices.

Rio nods. “Got it.”

The woman had turned back around too, ignoring Rio again. When the elevator doors open up, he exits with his phone back in hand, and the woman turns and gives Rio a brief and almost apologetic smile before leaving.

They enter their apartment as Rio walks towards the one next door. There was some twisted and poetic irony here: Rio didn’t need to resort to her safe space to not panic during the elevator ride. Turns out hot women are even better than a mindfulness technique.

From all the things that mark adulthood, going on a first-name basis with a former teacher was definitely one that can make someone feel terribly old. Lilia had been one of the first, maybe only, adults who tried to see Rio instead of just looking at her. Lilia almost got to see that sparkle of innocent potential only teenagers have.

After graduating high school, Rio tried to make Lilia’s efforts worth something by going to community college, but after one semester, Rio could no longer afford to live in the usual ways. So she turned to unusual ways to earn a living. It did turn out badly for everyone, including Lilia.

The memory made her cringe, and with a weird sense of debt, Rio bursts a confession. “Lilia, something happened today. I- I apparently took the wrong elevator, and one of the tenants got mad and said he would-”

Given a series of unfortunate events, Rio had a lot of How to Get Away With Murder episodes to binge, missing most of the final season. But her mind wasn’t allowing her to pay much attention, her mind traveling elsewhere.

Agatha. Nice name, right? Rio absently picked up her phone and googled the name. Good and honorable. Well, that elevator incident wasn't evidence of either of those.

But why now? She had ignored Rio every single time they were alone. Why in an elevator with her husband?

Entering and leaving without being noticed was an art that took years for Rio to craft. Chapter 2

Not many, of course. But since it began, her day had gone terribly bad. First, there was a tripping incident with some cables that nearly knocked out Lilia’s out-of-my-price-range crystal vase. Then, Rio discovered her recently purchased orbital sander wasn’t working.

And then, with a smile matching her eyes:

“That sounds dangerous, Agatha. I’ve… I’ve told her about you.”

“I didn't tell her that part, obviously. But she did know I was seeing my neighbor.”

“Oh. Oh. Well, Jen doesn’t know where I live. We’ll be fine.”

With a heavy feeling in her chest, Rio nods.

They eventually choose their clothes for Hiram’s party. Rio insists they return home immediately, but a very convincing Agatha wants hot chocolate. So, of course, they ended up sitting on a park bench watching children play.

When Agatha tries picking up her hand, Rio’s body jumps. “Rio, relax. Why do you think I’ve chosen to go out in another town?”

“We just bumped into my therapist, Agatha.”

Agatha sighs, dropping her hand. “Rio. You have to relax a bit, ok?”

Taking Agatha’s hand, Rio watches as the kids play together despite the cold. Her attention is caught by a kid running around in circles as fast as they can. Leaning over, Rio whispers, “That was me as a kid.”

“Trying to cause a hurricane?”

Rio laughs. “No. Being silly.”

Agatha smiles at her. She also looks around and points at a little girl reading a book underneath the shadow of a tree. “That was me.”

“Nerd?”

With a sad smile, Agatha shakes her head. “Alone.”

For the second time that week, Rio tries not to give in to the thoughts forming in her mind or the feeling of jealousy building inside her chest. Especially because picking up a possible fight or interrogation for being jealous of the dead didn’t seem to be a good way to spend their last day alone.

Her self-pity thoughts are interrupted when a little boy comes over to them and gives Rio a flower. “My lady,” he says.

Rio gasps. “For me? Thank you so much.”

The boy gestures for her to approach. “It’s magical,” he whispers. “Tiny people are living inside it.”

“Really? ‘Cause I can’t hear anythi-” Rio theatrically pauses, bringing the flower to her ear. “Wait. Oh. What did you say? I see…” she extends the flower back. “They want to talk with you, young man.”

He laughs, picking up the flower again and bringing it to his own ear just to run around again. Just then, Rio notices the look on Agatha’s face.

“What?”

“You’re good with kids.”

“I guess that’s what happens when you grow up in foster care,” she comments. “There are always younger kids that you have to learn to play with.”

“Do you want kids?” Agatha suddenly asks.

Caught off guard, Rio replies the first thing her mind came up with. “Not really. Or yes. Maybe someday. With the right person," she makes a quick pause, looking at Agatha. "Do you?”

Agatha shakes her head. “I would probably be a horrible mother.”

Rio chuckles sincerely. “You’re joking, right? You would be an amazing mother, Agatha.”

“Yeah, right.”

“No, I mean it. I’ve seen you with Ebony and Scratchy.”

“Not that I’m an expert, Rio, but I think dealing with a kid is different.”

“Of course it is. And I bet somehow you’ll be even better at raising our human kids,” Rio tries her luck.

For a moment, Agatha just freezes. Rio honestly expects her to run without a word, but instead she smiles before pulling Rio in for a long and tender kiss. “We'd better hope our plan works out then.”

Chapter End Notes

Who is Hiram Shaw?

Chapter 17

Chapter Notes

*taps the mic and sighs dramatically*

You won't believe what happened! Evanora Harkness came to my house , sneezed on my face and then just left, giving me a three month cold 😔 The things people do to delay their own murder, right?

No, but now seriously, the main reason I haven't updated this fic in so long is that I've (once again) quit smoking. So, sitting my ass down to write was a big NO for a while since my brain has connected writing and smoking. I've been writing my fics very slowly until my brain understands that "there is no cig coming, you bitch. It's just you and THE thoughts". The moral of the story is: don't drink and drive, but also don't smoke and write

I want to thank you all who are still here for your patience and support. Hopefully, this chapter won't suck because I did write it having in mind that it has been two whole months, so there might be a lot of unnecessary exposition to ease both the writing and the reading

Oh, btw, do you ever look at this Agatha and wonder what's going on inside her head? Well, you can read my Kinktober oneshot. There is no plot, but it's cute, and it's Agatha's pov!

If Rio ever wrote a poem as evidence of fate’s cruelty, it would be about falling asleep knowing Agatha was a thin wall away and still completely out of her reach. But as Rio was no poet, she settled for admitting to herself that her mattress was, indeed, awful.

There was a version of Rio who knew they couldn’t risk being caught now, but you are more than welcome to explain that to the current version of Rio, half asleep with Agatha gently playing with her hair. That version had no opinions about having to sneak through the Road’s back door like a dirty mistress.

Agatha’s voice is the first to break the comfortable silence.

“Now that my place is not an option because of Ralph, and yours is too risky because of Alice, I’ve been brainstorming some options.”

With a teasing smirk, Rio pulls away to look at Agatha. “Have you?”

“Of course. We need to find you a place to start painting as soon as possible if we want everything ready for the next month.”

Trying to hide the disappointment, Rio sits in bed with her arms around her legs. “Okay… Hm, maybe the greenhouse?”

Agatha snorts. “Are you joking? Mrs. Hart is one nosy bitch. She would find everything on day one. No. I do have a cottage, but the problem is that it’s one hour away, so it can be risky…”

“No, that sounds perfect.”

“Are you sure? Because Alice-”

“It’s only for a month, right? I can drive after work hours and return before sunrise. Besides, Alice trusts me.”

Rio can feel Agatha’s warm breathing come closer to her skin as Agatha starts kissing down her neck. “We can drive there tomorrow, then. Test your driving time skills. Among other skills…

“I have to help Mrs. Davis with the flower arrangements for Sunday. Remember?”

With that, Rio can practically feel Agatha’s body tensing up as the kisses stop.

“Something wrong?”

“No, nothing…” Agatha sits beside Rio and, after a moment, her voice is quieter than it should. “Mother will be there.”

“Okay…”

“I thought that maybe, after that dinner, you two being in the same room wouldn’t be the safest idea.”

“I’ll keep it cool, Agatha.” Then, unable to help herself. “As long as she doesn’t touch like that ever again, I won’t be-”

Out of the sudden, Agatha cups Rio’s face, brushing her fingers against her lower lip.

“Don’t.”

“Don’t what?”

“Don’t do anything reckless.”

Trying to guess where exactly that was coming from, Rio tilts her head and prepares to argue. But when blue eyes flicker from her eyes to her lips, there are only two words left in Rio’s vocabulary.

“Okay, Agatha.”

“We can go next Friday before Hiram’s party. You can ask Mrs. Hart to skip community service, can’t you?”

Rio couldn’t.

“I’ll talk to her, but… Are you sure you should go?”

“Why wouldn’t I?”

“I doubt your mother would react well if she found out.”

“Rio, mother doesn’t react well to anything. Especially when it comes to me.”

“I know, but we agreed on being cautious.”

Agatha sighs, gesturing to where they were. “We are being cautious, Rio. We haven’t seen each other in days. We haven’t even texted. You wouldn’t take my hand in public, even though we were out of town. It’s a fucking miracle that you agreed to meet me here instead of some shady side road motel.”

With a grunt, Agatha turns to her side.

“If you don’t want to take me, just say so.”

“What? That’s not… You know that’s not what I meant.”

There is a long pause before Agatha looks at Rio with a hurtful expression that doesn’t match the neutral tone of her next words. “If you decided I shouldn’t go, I can just wait for you at the cottage.”

And when Agatha turns to her side again, that proves one thing.

“Why are you mad?”

“I’m not mad.”

Liar.

Rio waits a few moments for Agatha to admit. When she doesn’t, Rio starts to stand to grab her shirt. But before her feet even touch the floor, Agatha grabs her by the wrist and pulls her in, wrapping Rio’s arms around herself.

Sniffing the lavender from Agatha’s hair, Rio closes her eyes as Agatha traces her violet tattoo. They just stay like that for a moment that feels way too short.

“Baby, I should leave.”

“You should.”

But neither of them moves.

“I’ll rest my eyes a little, okay? Then I’ll leave.”

“Hmm, okay,” Agatha answers in a sleepy voice. “Before Billy sees you.”

As it had been obvious to anyone, except them, ‘a little’ turned out to be the whole night. Rio didn’t even notice sleeping taking over her body. There were no dreams or nightmares. There was only Agatha.

And because fate is always cruel in the end, Rio awakens with a loud noise coming from outside the office. When the clock announced that the Road was closed for three hours now, and with Agatha sleeping peacefully beside her, Rio knew that there was an unwelcome presence among them.

Careful not to make a sound, Rio put on Agatha’s oversized shirt she used as pajamas and grabbed the first object that could hopefully be used as a blunt weapon: a heavy purple lamp.

The grinding sound gets louder as Rio walks through the dark hallway that leads to the bar. Looking at the expensive lamp, Rio considers that calling the cops would be a much better plan against whatever weapon the burglars were using.

Rio’s knuckles turn white as she hears someone hiding behind the counter.

“Fuck!”

The sound stops completely at the same time that Rio recognizes the voice.

“Billy?”

A gothic head emerges as Billy stands with one hand brushing his head. “Hi, Rio!”

“What are you doing here?”

“Coffee?” He points at the coffee grinder. “I broke a mug and hit my head while cleaning. Sorry. Did I wake you up?”

“Well, yeah. I was still trying to…”

That’s when the penny drops.

Rio was standing at the Witches’ Road bar. Half-naked, wearing only Agatha's shirt. Holding Agatha’s purple lampshade. While Agatha was still sleeping in the office.

“Shit… Dude, this is not what it looks like. Agatha asked me if I could-”

Billy doesn’t even stop making his coffee. “It doesn’t look like anything, Rio.”

Rio puts the lamp on the counter and sits down on the stool.

“It doesn’t?”

Billy shakes his head.

“I’ve known Agatha long enough not to ask questions and well enough to keep my mouth shut.” Billy smiles at her. “As far as I know, you were never here.”

Despite blushing a bit, Rio tries returning his smile, muttering a quiet, “Thank you, Billy.”

“You’re welcome. If it helps, I never really bought that whole ‘break up’ thing.”

“Really? And why is that?”

“Agatha is in a good mood way too often. And since you two started to… You know. Well, that office hasn’t seen it’s usual parade of wome-”

Rio can feel her face turning red for a completely different reason as Bily stops talking immediately.

“I’ll just shut up,” he concludes.

“Yeah…”

“Hey, maybe you could, you know, convince Agatha to give me a raise? You know, for how cool I’m being right now.”

In anyone else’s voice, those words would sound like a clear threat. But in Billy’s? It sounded like the sweetest request ever made. “I’ll talk to her.”

With the adrenaline rushing down, Rio takes a second to look around. The bar was clean and tidy. A shy smell of lemon bleach mixes with the coffee.

“Not to sound rude, but why are you still here?”

“Oh, I sleep in Agatha’s office when I work in the garden. It’s closer and easier than going home to sleep for three hours.” Billy pours coffee into two mugs, then he adds in a whisper. “Plus, I can use Agatha’s expensive grains for free.”

“Saving money on coffee, uh?”

Billy shrugs. “Teddy and I are saving for our own place next year, so every penny counts, right?”

Assuming Teddy was the boyfriend Billy had been talking about nonstop since they met, Rio nods and accepts the mug, feeling the warmth in her palms.

“Where did you sleep tonight?”

Billy’s gaze drifts to a corner couch with a clumsily folded blanket and a pillow.

“It’s cozier than it looks, I promise,” he says, recognizing the pity look Rio gave him. “Aren’t you going to drink it?”

Rio shakes her head. “Not a fan.”

“Are you sure? We’ll have to be at the garden in less than an hour. We won’t leave until five.”

And Rio practically pours the coffee down her throat.

They ended up even brewing some more coffee. Rio even tried waking up Agatha to say goodbye, but the woman was passed out and only mumbled a few incomprehensible words before falling asleep again. Rio made a mental note to mock her later because who was the heavy sleeper now? She also left a physical post-it note along with a goodbye kiss.

The whole morning is spent on arrangements and Billy’s Pinterest boards for his first apartment with Teddy.

It still sounded disgustingly cute.

Rio gave him some tips on apartment hunting and decorating a rental.

And it still was surprisingly easy to talk with Billy. He had enough charisma for Rio to handle a whole day with him, but not that forced sympathy that would give Rio a headache. Billy never seemed to mind doing all the small talk, which meant RIo didn’t have to talk about herself.

But most importantly, Billy knew when to stop talking and let some room for silence. After a few moments with just Billy’s music playing in the background, Rio let her mind wander why exactly a twenty-one-year-old would end up in a community garden after a night shift in a lesbian bar.

“How did you get this gig with Mrs. Davis?”

It takes him some unusual time to answer, “I’m testing some hobbies, you know? Trying a bit of everything until I learn what I’m good at,” he says, clumsily trying to put together witchhazels and paperwhites. “I’ve been trying the guitar. Teddy likes it, but Tommy says I sound like a ‘bunch of cats in heat having a collective brain stroke during a heat wave’. So, I think that means I might be bad at it.”

Rio nods, trying to imagine what that would sound like. “And you're into gardening?”

“Honestly, I don’t even like gardening, but Mrs. Davis is so sweet, I don’t have the nerve to tell her that.”

“You spend every Sunday here, and you don’t even like gardening?”

“I know.”

“Billy…”

“I know, Rio.”

“You have to tell Mrs. Davis that you don’t like gardening.”

Billy releases a loud sigh. “She’ll be so upset. And it’s temporary. My mom wants me to try a few colleges next year, so I won’t be able to keep helping her here and working at the Road.”

Rio tries not to sound too excited by the Wanda mention. “And is your mom okay with you working at the bar?”

Billy swallows dry, looking behind his shoulder before continuing in a whisper. “Mom doesn’t know, okay?”

“Where does she think you go five nights a week?”

“She thinks I’m in Tommy’s place or with Teddy.”

“Wow. Lying to your parents, uh?”

“Don’t. I already feel bad enough. But mom would freak out if she knew I’m working on the Road.”

“Because of her whole mysterious fight with Agatha?”

“Yeah, that too. But my mom also wants me to have a serious job with a serious future. That sort of stuff all moms want for their children. You know how that goes, right?”

“Sure do,” Rio lies, having no idea what that meant.

With the expectation of Lilia, mother figures weren’t exactly Rio’s field of knowledge. Rio was about to ask another question when Mrs. Davis walks in with her phone in hand and voice higher than usual.

“If there are no other options, I’ll find another way, but know this, young man, I’m very, very disappointed at you. I’ll be speaking about this with your mother.”

Mrs. Davis hangs off and sits on the couch with her head resting on her hands.

“What’s wrong?” Rio asks.

“The crew we hired for tomorrow’s party canceled last minute. Can you believe that? So unprofessional. We won’t be able to finish any of the decorations in time, so we might just as well cancel the party.”

“Can’t you do the party without the decorations?”

Mrs. Davis gasps, personally offended by Rio’s lack of education on building party planning. Having the party canceled wouldn’t be bad, actually. She could go back home, and maybe Agatha would be free. They could spend the whole weekend on the Road doing all the-

“We’ll help,” Billy offers. “Right, Rio?”

Before Rio can even shoot him an ugly look, Mrs. Davis is already coming towards them. “Will you? Oh, that’s wonderful. Let me just grab my notebook so we can split shores.”

When Mrs. Davis leaves, Rio finally gives him the look.

“Dude, you don’t even like helping with the arrangements…”

Billy makes a hush sound. Not much later, Mrs. Davis returned with her notebook and five different colors of pens. They spent the rest of their day finishing the arrangements and splitting tasks for tomorrow. Mrs. Davis even invited them for lunch as a thank-you gesture. The fact that she left them no room to deny said lunch wasn’t lost on Rio either.

So, one painfully sleepless night later, Rio went straight to pick up the arrangements and came back to find Billy waiting for her at the lobby. He had been kind enough to even bring his own toolbox. Rio was quite proud.

Their morning was mostly spent trying to solve Mrs. Davis’s handwriting, but they eventually managed everything. Rio even found the time to adjust one of the chairs that had a loose screw. Rio would probably need to warn Mrs. Davis that the screw needed to be changed and fixed properly, but she did the best she could at the moment.

When they were done, Mrs. Davis was waiting for them with lunch ready, only cutting vegetables for the salad.

“Are you sure you don’t want to stay for the party, Billy?” Mrs. Davis asks while they were both sitting in her kitchen. “You can bring Teddy.”

“We sort of have plans…” he says, trying to hide the fact that he had zero interest in staying for a party with older people.

Knowing Mrs. Davis she was about to suggest he stay anyway. Knowing Billy, Rio decides to help him out for once. “Speaking of plans, Mrs. Davis. I was wondering if I could work next Friday instead of Saturday.”

“Are you also going to Lorna Wu’s tribute concert?” Billy asks.

“What? No, I haven’t even heard her stuff yet… I have plans with a friend.”

Mrs. Davis gives her a little grin. “You kids and your friends. Sure, Rio. You can come next Friday.”

After some insistence, Billy ended up playing Lorna’s discography as Mrs. Davis gave the lunch its final touches, and Rio helped by setting the table.

“Lorna’s music was one of the best things that ever happened in the music industry.”

“Did you know I went to Lorna’s very first concert?”

Even though she wasn’t in the room with them, Rio could practically see Billy’s eyes lightening up.

“No way! How?”

“Lorna and I were members of the same student movements in college, if you must know. We had lots of friends in common back then. We kept in touch until…”

Rio smiles, imagining a younger Mrs. Davis as part of political groups in the 70s.

“That fire was a tragedy. She was so young- Mrs. Davis, your finger!”

Sprinting to the kitchen, Rio finds Billy holding a blood-soaked cloth against Mrs. Davis’s right hand. “What happened?”

“We need to take her to the hospital!”

“No hospitals. It was just a superficial cut, you two.”

“Are you sure? I can grab the car in two seconds.”

“I’m sure, Rio. Can you grab the emergency kit in the bathroom?”

Rio starts opening the doors in the hallway like a madwoman. The first led to what appeared to be an office. The second one, however, made Rio stop at her feet.

Definitely not a bathroom.

Even with the curtains closed, the dim light coming from the hallway reveals enough for Rio to realize where she was.

She should turn around, close the door and grab that emergency kit in the bathroom.

That should be the plan.

That’s what Rio should have done.

But something familiar on the dresser catches her eye.

Shutting the door behind her, Rio turns on the light with a heavy feeling building inside her chest. Rio walks towards the framed picture of a blonde girl with her arms wrapped around a brunette girl. She shouldn’t be more than fifteen, but Rio could recognize her blue eyes anywhere.

Agatha’s hair was shorter and somehow even more wild, but her face was happier. Maybe more peaceful than Rio had ever seen her. The sort of innocent happiness that only youth brings to someone.

Rio lifts her eyes to take a better look at where she was. Decorated in different shades of pink, the bedroom had stars on the ceiling that had lost their glow. The walls were filled with drawings. One particular canvas was bigger than the rest and centered. Definitely more important than others. And Rio could easily recognize.

A murder of crows descending into runes.

Putting the frame back, Rio walks towards the drawing and picks up the canvas from the wall. And something falls from behind it.

A pile of letters tied together with a purple thread.

“Rio? Did you find it?” Billy asks from the kitchen.

“Yeah, just… Just a minute.”

In a move that Rio isn’t either proud or conscious, Rio puts the letters inside her coat’s pocket and leaves the bedroom, making sure to put the canvas back where it belong.

The rest of lunch is a blur. Rio wasn’t entirely aware of Billy putting a bandage around Mrs. Davis’s finger. Or Rio putting the emergency kit back in the bathroom cabinet. Rio didn’t even know she could answer what they ate at lunch. Or when exactly she got home.

All Rio knew was that she was sitting in the living room with two piles of letters. The two were strangely the same. Yellowish paper that indicates time had passed. The same purple threads carefully wrapped around it.

For a moment, Rio can only stare at it as if both piles might go off like a bomb. When Rio finally unwraps the thread from the most recently stolen pile and opens the first one, it feels like the world’s most awkward deja vu.

The words were strangely similar. The same love confessions, promises, secrets. The same stories filled those pages as much as the letters Rio had found in Ebony’s stash.

But it was the differences that caught her attention. Curse words. Teenage slang. Unfiltered stories. Crossed out words. Horrible handwriting. Grammar mistakes. The main difference between those two piles was that the second didn’t feel staged.

It felt like Agatha.

And it felt much more painful to read.

So, when her alarm went off, Rio stashed both piles in the kitchen cabinet right beside the morning cereal, took a shower and got dressed. By the time she walked out to the hallway and started walking towards the staircase, Rio considers a plan for how she could put the letters back in Mrs. Davis’s apartment and find her way into putting the letters back inside Agatha’s couch before-

“Rio!” Ralph’s voice snaps her out of her plans. “New suit?”

“No, it was a gift from… a friend,” Rio lies, stopping by their front door.

“Well, you look gorgeous.” They both hear a scoff and turn to see Agatha leaning against the doorframe. Ralph instantly puts his hands on Agatha’s waist. “But not as gorgeous as my wife.”

And Rio would now be the one needing to contain her own disdain if it wasn’t for her eyes immediately dropping in Agatha’s dark purple velvet dress. The one bought specifically to match her suit.

“You do look… gorgeous.”

Knowing her well enough, Rio notices how Agatha forces herself to bite down the smile.

Ralph clears his throat, playfully gesturing at his own suit and suggestively looking at Rio.

“You look… less gorgeous, Ralph, but the suit is really nice.”

“Oh yeah, that’s what I wanted to hear,” he answers, tapping Rio’s shoulder.

Agatha rolls her eyes, unteangling herself from Ralph and marching down the hallway.

“We’re late.”

They both follow Agatha towards the elevator. This time, it doesn’t even cross Rio’s mind to take the stairs as she takes her place in the back while both Ralph and Agatha stay at the front. The doors briefly close and reopen on the floor below.

Mrs. Davis’s bright smile slowly drops when her eyes go from Rio to Agatha to Ralph and back again. Ralph uses his arm to prevent the door from closing.

“Are you coming?”

Mrs. Davis blinks away the embarrassment. “The elevator seems full,” she says, stepping away. “I’ll just take the next one.”

Ralph frowns, but removes his arm, and as the door closes once again, he mutters under his breath, “What a crazy bitch.”

Rio and Agatha exchange a knowing look as they try not to laugh.

The whole thing resembles their first meeting at that same elevator months ago. Only this time, Rio’s pull towards Agatha feels inevitable and her fingers gently touch her hair.

The touch is soft enough for Agatha not to feel, but a moan rips through her lips all the same. Loud enough for Rio to hear it and, unfortunately, public enough for Ralph.

Agatha instantly covers it with a yawn.

“Are you alright, honey?”

Rio takes a step back, leaning against the wall.

“Just tired.”

“Again? Oh, no. Is your insomnia back? I thought you had fixed that problem, dear.”

Rio can feel Agatha’s eyes dropping on her for a split second.

“So did I.”

“I’ll ask my assistant to schedule you an appointment with our doctor.”

“No, I have a feeling it will go away on its own next month.”

Having caught that reference, Rio cannot help but smile at that.

If asked what the highest form of intimacy is, most people would answer something like having sex or seeing someone naked. Some might even argue that kissing was a far more intimate activity than sex. But it didn’t matter anyway because none of them were right.

For Rio, sharing a secret with someone that no one else in the world could even imagine was the highest level of intimacy two people could ever achieve. Knowing that something was about to happen without anyone else’s knowledge was far more intimate than having Agatha moaning in her ear.

As it turns out, murder creates a sense of partnership that not even being naked on top of each other could ever provide.

And as you cannot think of the devil without manifesting it, when the elevator doors opened up, Evanora Harkness was standing there.

“Mother, we were about to-”

“And I was about to see why it was taking you two so long,” she interrupts. “I’ve been waiting at the looby for five whole minutes, Agatha.”

Evanora stares at Agatha, waiting for an apology that doesn’t come.

“I’m sorry, Evanora. It won’t happen again,” Ralph says, stepping out of the elevator.

It sure won’t.

Even though Evanora doesn’t acknowledge Rio’s presence, her eyes are fixed on her as she takes a small box from her purse and gives it to Ralph.

“I’ve got you a new one, Ralph. Make sure not to lose it this time.”

Rio watches as Ralph takes a ring from the box. One similar to Agatha’s. Similar to the many that Rio had found in Ebony’s stash, but it holds a completely different meaning now. Rio tries catching Agatha’s reaction, but again, there was none.

Evanora gives Agatha one final look, “You’re repeating dresses now, Agatha? So unpleasant of you. Remind me to ask Vertigo to send you some new dresses.”

“I like this one.”

“Well, that’s clearly why I need to step in, don’t you agree?”

Letting the three of them go ahead, Rio waits at the lobby for Mrs. Davis. The party was the same as all the previous ones, which meant Rio was instantly bored. While Agatha and Ralph entertained Evanora from across the room, Rio remained glued to Mrs. Davis for most of the part.

“Can you believe she had the nerve to show up?” Mrs. Davis whispered. “Uninvited as if being here was her birthright.”

It wasn’t until much later that Rio heard a voice behind her.

“Rio! How have you been?”

“Hiram? What- What are you doing here?”

“Why? Wasn’t I supposed to?”

“No, no. You can do what-”

“I’m just messing with you, Rio,” he taps her shoulder. “I’ve heard you finished renovating Agatha’s apartment. I must say I was surprised by how little time it took.”

Rio swallows dry. “How did you know?”

“Ralph is home.”

Finally, looking at the other side of the room, Rio watches as the three stare back at them. Especially at how Evanora’s face is as pale as a ghost. Agatha seemed frozen. Ralph was starting to walk towards them.

“Speaking of the devil,” Hiram says, raising his glass to him. “Ralph!”

“Sir, what do we owe the pleasure?” Ralph asks, clearly surprised and upset. “We thought we weren’t going to see you until next month."

“Well, I was at home drinking alone, and then I remember, ‘Hey, I do have free will’. And I must add, a very malleable schedule,” Hiram answers. “Is there a problem, Ralph?”

“No, not at all. We’ve just been wondering if you would like to join us-” “I would not.”

“Are you sure, sir? I don’t wish to leave you alone all night.”

“Well, in case you haven't noticed: Rio and I are talking.”

“Rio and… Oh.”

Ralph freezes, looking at Rio as if she were his savior.

“I can keep him company,” she offers.

When Ralph unwillingly left, Rio glanced at Evanora, staring at them with fury written all over her face.

“So, Rio, how is the other renovation going?”

“I’m almost done there too.”

“Oh, and you’ll be looking for new jobs?”

“No, not at the moment, sir. I… I’m still thinking where I’ll go when it’s finished since Lilia is selling the apartment and-”

“So you’re moving out?”

Rio nods.

“That’s terrible. I’m sure you’ll be missed here, my dear,” Hiram says, looking at Agatha. “I’ve actually come here with a purpose in mind.”

“Did you? What purpose?”

“I haven’t received your confirmation.”

“My confirmation?”

“To the party next Saturday, of course.”

“Oh, I wasn’t aware I was supposed to send one.”

“It’s common courtesy. I think it’s unnecessary, but my wife believes it’s important. Just give me your full name and your plus one.”

“I won’t be bringing anyone, sir.”

“Oh, really?” Hiram absently says. “I was under the impression you would be bringing Agatha.”

“Agatha?”

“Yes. You two are friends, aren’t you?”

“I… I wouldn’t say ‘friends’, really.”

“Nonsense, Rio. I’m glad that Agatha has a… friend. It’s important to have the right people around you, isn’t it? I never quite understood the saying ‘keep your enemies closer’. Why would I want them anywhere near me?”

Rio nods. “Right.”

“So, what do you say?”

“I’m not sure if she’ll-” Rio looks at Evanora. “I don’t want to cause her any trouble, sir.”

Hiram follows Rio's eyes. “Then let’s make sure you won’t cause her any trouble,” he says, opening a devious grin.

And without even allowing Rio to say anything, Hiram entangles their arms together and starts walking toward Agatha.

“Agatha, my darling,” he greets, giving her a warm hug.

Evanora seems about to have a heart attack. Which, honestly, would save everyone so much trouble.

“Nice seeing you.”

“Glad someone here thinks so,” Hiram says. “Tell me, dear, are you free next weekend?”

“If I’m free… Why do you ask?”

“Well, my wife has been insisting I throw a birthday party for myself, and I was wondering if you-”

“A birthday party?” Evanora cuts him off. “Oh, Hiram. Hasn’t your wife planned you enough of those?”

Hiram finally looks at her, giving her a smirk. “I’ve heard it's less expensive than planning a funeral,” he deadpans before returning his attention to Agatha. “It would mean the world to me if you were there.”

Agatha opens her mouth to answer, but Evanora is quicker. “Agatha and I have plans for next weekend.”

“Do we?” Agatha asks.

“Come on, Evanora. I’m sure Agatha can be released from the prison you built for her for two days, can’t she?”

Evanora looks at Rio with the corner of her eye.

“What are you even doing here?”

Rio opens her mouth to answer, but Hiram puts pressure on her arm.

“Don’t be like that, Evanora. It’s one weekend.”

Evanora puts herself back together. “Well, I’m sure Ralph and Agatha will be delighted to attend if-”

Hiram smirks. “Ralph is not invited.”

“That’s absurd, Hiram. Ralph is her husband.”

Evanora looks at Ralph, who was still holding his whiskey, as he puts his arms around Agatha as if proving her point.

“It’s not absurd. I’ve already said: it’s a small gathering. I’m sure Ralph understands that there won’t be any room for him. Right, Ralph?”

Rio can see a small drop of sweat falling down Ralph’s forehead as he looks at Evanora. “Sure, Hiram, I’ll-”

“Call me ‘sir’,” he corrects.

“Sorry. Sir,” Ralph says, clearly nervous.

Then it happens. Ralph gets clumsy and spills his drink on Agatha’s dress. More specifically, in her cleavage.

“Fuck, Ralph!”

“Agatha, language!” Evanora scolds.

“Sorry, Agatha. I didn’t mean to…” Ralph says, grabbing his handkerchief and bringing it closer to Agatha.

Rio wants to bite him.

“Stop it, Ralph. I can do it myself.”

“So, we’re settled for next weekend, right, Agatha?”

Not paying much attention. Agatha nods. “Of course. I’ll just go to the bathroom to clean this up. Otherwise, it will stain the velvet.”

When Agatha leaves, Evanora looks at Hiram, ready to snap.

“I cannot believe you’ll force Agatha into one of your nonsenses, Hiram,” Evanora says. “That’s why this hasn’t been-”

Her words are interrupted by the sound of a chair breaking and someone falling to the ground. A whole crowd forms around the elder man as Mrs. Davis quickly comes to meet Rio.

“Rio, can you…?”

“Yeah. I’ll grab a screwdriver really quick.”

Rio promptly starts walking towards the exit and instantly hears clicking clack heels.

“Ms. Vidal?” the voice sends chills down Rio’s spine.

Rio walks even faster, controlling herself not to run towards the elevator.

“Ms. Vidal!” Evanora repeats louder and sharper, her voice leaving no room for interpretation.

It was a demand.

Rio feels someone grabbing her forearm.

“Sorry, Mrs. Harkness. I didn’t hear you.”

“Right. Now that you did, can I have a word?”

“I really have to get the screwdriver before… someone else gets hurt.”

“Someone has already got hurt, Ms. Vidal.”

After a moment of them just staring at each other, Evanora presses the elevator button without ever letting go of Rio’s arm.

“I’ll walk with you to your apartment.”

They step into the elevator. As the door closes, Rio shuts her eyes and leans against the wall once again. In her list of worst things that could happen, being stuck with Evanora Harkness in an elevator was surprisingly at the top.

The silence was loud and uncomfortable, and when they reached their destination, Rio left as if it was her mission on earth to get as far as possible from Evanora. She drops her keys trying to unlock the door as her hands were shaking from the adrenaline. If from the elevator ride or Evanroa’s presence, she wasn’t entirely sure.

Evanora gently takes the keys from her hands. “Allow me, Ms. Vidal.”

Rio opens the door and walks in while Evanora evaluates the place from the threshold. “May I come in?”

Rio nods, grabbing a water bottle and drinking nonstop.

“Are you alright, Ms. Vidal? You seem… Distressed.”

“I’m fine,” the last word came out as a spit. So, she tries faking a smile. “Not used to taking the elevator.”

Evanora nods and gives Rio a fragile smile that doesn’t match the image Rio had of her.

“I had panic attacks nearly every day when Agatha was a child. Motherhood can be quite an experience. Do you plan on having children, Ms. Vidal?”

“What? No, I… Why?”

“Are you and your mother close?”

“No.”

“Terribly sorry to hear that.”

“I’m not,” Rio says abruptly. “Being present without wanting to be can be worse than leaving.”

Evanora looks at the hallway as if remembering something. And, after a moment, she nods. “I suppose you can say that. But there are things that you cannot judge unless you’re a mother yourself.”

With that, Rio starts searching for her toolbox to get out there as soon as possible and avoid saying more things that can get her in trouble.

“Has Agatha ever told you we used to own this unit as well?”

“No,” Rio half-lies because, despite knowing, Agatha had never told her.

And then, out of nowhere, which is usually where realizations like this come from, Rio notices the amount of second-hand information that she has on Agatha. Mostly from Mrs. Davis and Billy, but very little is from Agatha herself.

“Agatha’s father insisted we buy this apartment. I hated it. I thought it was small and old, then he bought the one next door. We decided to demolish the walls connecting the two apartments. It led to a fantastic master bedroom, you know?” Evanora says as if Rio were paying attention. “When we moved, we were quite in a hurry, so everything was done so… poorly. Lilia didn’t mind when she bought this one since there was no one living next door. Now, tell me, Ms. Vidal, was that a problem?”

“What was a problem?”

Evanora was standing with Lilia’s crystal vase in hand. Rio’s body tenses at the sight. Evanora must have noticed because she smiles down at the vase. “The construction workers warned me that the quickie renovation could mean there would be less material on the walls. So, it could lead to some echo and the ability to hear the other room.”

Rio stops at her feet.

“I haven’t heard anything,” Rio lies.

“Oh, that’s such a relief.”

Rio takes the vase from Evanora’s hands and carefully places it on the table again.

“I like what you’ve done with the place. You are very talented, Ms. Vidal.” Rio nods, opening the toolbox and searching for the screwdriver.

“Shit… I can’t find the screwdriver.”

“You can’t find it? How do you work without a screwdriver?”

“I must have left…”

Fuck.

“You must have left…?”

“In Ralph’s apartment.”

Evanora smiles. “You left your tools in your client’s house? I suppose that’s quite unusual, right?”

“No. It’s not. I just… I’ll text him and-”

“Don’t bother. I do have the code to unlock the front door. Come.”

When Evanora turns on the light, Ebony comes meowing towards them and stops at her paws when she sees Evanora there, spinning around and leaving immediately.

“So, where did you leave it?”

“I’m not sure… Maybe the office? I’ll go check-”

“Please. Allow me. Can’t have you entering rooms unauthorized, can we? I’ll look for it.”

When Evanora leaves, Rio sits on the couch.

“It’s not there. Where else can it be?”

Rio fears the answer to this question.

“I have no idea.”

Because if not in Ralph’s office, Agatha must have taken and placed it where it usually was: her nightstand.

“We’ll just have to call it off and-”

Evanora sighs. “I’ll take one from them. You can give them back later.”

When Evanora vanishes into the dark hallway, Ebony comes sprinting and sits in Rio’s lap, meowing loudly.

“I’ve missed you too,” Rio whispers, kissing Ebony’s forehead. “Now go, baby.”

But Ebony doesn’t move.

“Ebony, I’m serious: you have to go.”

Rio hears footsteps approaching.

“Ebony, please…”

Rio tries pushing the cat away from her lap and, when realizing it's a lost battle, she does something that breaks her heart. Rio hisses at Ebony.

Ebony looks at her with a confused expression and runs away through the dark hallway. Rio can hear Evanora cursing as Ebony passes running towards her.

“That cat is a menace,” Evanora comments, handing Rio the screwdriver. “Here. You can return this later.”

“Thank you,” Rio says unwillingly. “Can we go?”

“Not so fast, Ms. Vidal. We still haven’t talked. Do you want anything to drink?”

“No.”

But Evanora is not worried about time. She slowly opens a bottle of brandy and pours a glass. She puts the ice inside the glass and crushes it with an ice picker.

Rio wonders how easily it would be to put the ice picker into Evanora’s neck.

Don’t do anything reckless.

The hag was already sitting on the couch. “So, Ralph was telling me about the work you’ve done here, Ms. Vidal. I must say I was nearly… disappointed. Especially seeing what you’ve done in Lilia’s place.”

“I had more time to work on her place.”

Evanora nods. “I suppose. Look, Ms. Vidal, since you’re in a hurry, I’ll be quick. About what you’ve seen that night at dinner-”

Rio shuts her eyes. “You do not have to apologize for that, Mrs. Harkness,” Rio lies.

“I wasn’t going to, Ms. Vidal,” Evanora says, standing and grabbing a few notes out of her purse. There wasn’t even sixty dollars there. “I would like to pay you for your assistance. I’m aware Ralph and Agatha have been using your help without rewarding you for it.”

“That’s not necessary.”

“Oh, but it is. We must recompense those who work for us.”

“With all due respect, Mrs. Harkness, I do not work for you.”

Evanora comes closer. “Take the money, Ms. Vidal. If you don’t, I’ll have to start worrying about you. We wouldn’t like that, would we?”

Rio considers taking the money just to keep Evanora happy for the little time she had on this Earth. But taking would also prove her point: everything and anyone could be bought.

And Rio wasn't up to sell.

"You don’t have to worry about me, Mrs. Harkness," Rio whispers. "If Agatha or Ralph want to recompensate me financially, they are welcome to do it themselves, but I don't mind if they don't. Not everything is about money."

Evanora steps back and Rio starts walking away.

"Are you sure, Ms. Vidal? It would be such a tragedy if you happens to be wrong."

But Rio is already shutting the door and running downstairs.

When she got back to the party, Rio could feel Agatha giving her a puzzling look when she saw Rio returning with Evanora right behind.

After the party was over, Rio was almost asleep when she heard footsteps in the hallway.

Rio sits in bed and curses herself for not having a knife under her pillow. She stands and waits behind the door. When the intruder walks into her bedroom, Rio grabs them from behind, twisting their arms on their back.

“Fuck, Rio! That hurts.”

“Agatha? What are you-”

But her words are interrupted by Agatha hitting her.

“What was that for?!”

Another hit followed by a sudden pull as Agatha hugs Rio tightly.

“Agatha? What the fuck happened?”

“You shouldn’t have left with her, Rio!”

“What? Why are you hitting me because of that?”

“Don’t you ever do that again. Do you understand?”

Taking Agatha’s hands, Rio forces her to sit beside her in bed.

“Agatha… Baby, you’re not being very… rational right now.”

“Oh! I’m not being rational?” Agatha asks angrily. Then, hits Rio once again with the pillow. “I’m not being rational? Of course I’m not, Rio. I came back, and Ralph said you and my mother left. Do you have any idea what I was thinking?!”

“Agatha, I won’t murder your mother during a party with dozens of witnesses!"

“That’s not what I…. Don’t do that again."

"Leave a party?"

"Be alone with her, Rio!"

From the way Agatha was breathing heavily and with tears forming in her eyes, Rio could only nod.

“I promise.”

Agatha nods, taking deep breaths. “Good. Now, I should go.”

“You should.”

But again, neither of them moves.

At some point, Rio slowly lies Agatha down in bed and holds her tightly, placing kisses on the top of her head.

“I’m sorry,” Agatha mumbles quietly. "For hitting you..."

“It’s okay. You were worried… It’s kind of cute.”

Agatha laughs, turning to face Rio. “Yeah?”

“Yeah…”

Rio kisses the tip of her nose and Agatha's laugh fills the dark room. When Rio starts playing with Agatha's hair, it doesn't take much for Agatha to be fast asleep, with Rio following her shortly after.

Apologies to fate because maybe her mattress wasn’t so bad afterall, as long as Agatha was lying there too.

When Rio woke up the next morning, Agatha was gone. The only trace she was ever there was the mess she left on the other side of the bed and one single post-it with nothing but a purple heart.

enjoyed their work!
